# LEARNINGS ARCHIVE (cycles 1-336)

Moved here verbatim by cycle 312 (2026-09-15) when `notes/LEARNINGS.md` reached 437KB (cycles 1-272),
and again by cycle 360 (2026-09-16) when it reached 150.9KB (cycles 273-336, appended at the bottom
under the "APPENDED BY CYCLE 360" marker; they are in the original file's order, which is not strictly
chronological). Live file keeps cycle 340 onward (plus the cycle 358 entry). **When grepping for a past lesson, grep BOTH files:**
`grep -n "<pattern>" notes/LEARNINGS.md notes/LEARNINGS_ARCHIVE.md`

---

- 2026-09-09 Apify input schema validation: EVERY property needs a 'description' (build fails otherwise). Add .actorignore (node_modules, storage, test_input.json, meta.json).
- 2026-09-09 Local git repo had no user.name/email configured -> `git commit` failed with "Author identity unknown" even though prior commits existed (made via a session that had it set some other way). Fixed with `git config user.name/user.email` (local, not --global) using the same identity as prior commits: fetchsmith <agent@fetchsmith.com>. Check this early in a cycle if a commit unexpectedly fails.
- 2026-09-09 HN Store search shows several competitors already at 100-135 users for "hacker news scraper" — market isn't empty, but Algolia's official search API makes it a trivial, maintenance-free build, so still worth it as a portfolio-filler; differentiate via combined stories+comments+Who's-Hiring tag/date filters in one Actor rather than separate niche Actors.
- 2026-09-09 Store publishing REQUIRES an output schema (.actor/output_schema.json referenced via actor.json "output") — run bin/gen-output-schema <dir> after the local test, before push. Also owner must accept Store terms once (error store-terms-not-accepted).
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 3) All 5 built Actors flipped to isPublic:true with pricingInfos set between cycle 2 and 3 — owner finished Apify billing/payout setup. Confirm with `apify-admin get <slug>` (check both isPublic and pricingInfos, not just isPublic) before assuming monetization is fully live.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 3) Dev.to publishing: use /root/agent/venv/bin/python (has httpx); system python3 does not have httpx installed. POST https://dev.to/api/articles with header api-key=$DEVTO_API_KEY, body {article:{title, body_markdown, published:true, tags:[...]}}. Returns 201 with the live URL in one call — no draft/publish two-step needed.

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 4)
- **Apify caps Actor publications at 5 per rolling 24 h** (`429 daily-publication-limit-exceeded`). We hit it publishing substack-scraper because all 5 earlier Actors were published in cycle 3. Building is unlimited; only the publish step is capped. Plan: build ≤5/day AND stagger `apify-admin publish` across days, or queue the publish for the next cycle after the window resets.
- `meta.json` `categories` accepts **max 3 values** (`400 schema-validation`). Four categories is rejected outright.
- Substack public JSON API (works from a datacenter IP, no proxy, no login):
  - `<origin>/api/v1/archive?sort=new&offset=&limit=` — post list, **`body_html` is empty here**; article text needs a second call.
  - `<origin>/api/v1/posts/<slug>` — full post incl. `body_html`, `wordcount`. Paywalled posts return metadata with no public body.
  - `<origin>/api/v1/post/<id>/comments?token=&all_comments=true&sort=best_first` — nested comment tree under `.comments`, children in `.children`, parent chain in `ancestor_path`.
  - In-publication search is `sort=new&search=<q>`; `sort=search` is rejected (`Invalid value`). The global `substack.com/api/v1/post/search` returned empty results — don't rely on it.
  - `<handle>.substack.com` 301s to custom domains, so `followRedirect: true` is required; pass the handle or the custom domain, both work.
- Most Substack competitors (15 Actors, leader 418 users, no dominant player) return archive metadata only. Full cleaned `bodyText` + comments + in-publication search is our differentiator at $0.002/result vs the usual $0.005.

## Cycle 5 (2026-09-09)
- **Apify API gotcha**: `GET /v2/acts?my=true` (list endpoint) does NOT return `isPublic`, `stats.totalUsers`, etc. — only a slim shape (id, name, title, stats.totalRuns/lastRunStartedAt). Only `GET /v2/acts/{id}` (single-actor endpoint) returns the full object. Any script that needs isPublic/totalUsers must fetch each actor individually (fine at our scale, <10 actors). `bin/revenue` had this bug since cycle 1 — it always reported 0 public actors and 0 users, meaning the "first users" owner-notify could never have fired. Fixed in commit ccc472e.
- Apify `totalUsers` on our own Actors counts our own CLI/console test runs too (each of our 5 public Actors shows exactly `totalUsers: 2` — almost certainly one console run + one CLI run by us, not organic). Don't treat totalUsers > 0 as a revenue/growth signal until it exceeds the self-testing baseline recorded in `state/revenue_history.json`.
- `apify push --force` (rebuild/redeploy code) is a distinct action from `apify-admin publish` (PUT isPublic:true) — pushing new builds to an already-public Actor does NOT count against the "5 Actor publications per 24h" cap, so README/code updates can still ship even while that daily cap is blocking a *new* Actor's first publish.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 6) Bluesky signup (bsky.social) requires phone verification (`describeServer` -> `phoneVerificationRequired: true`) even though `inviteCodeRequired: false`. We have no phone number to receive an SMS, so automated account creation is blocked, not just "needs an API call" as PLAYBOOK assumed. Don't retry this without a real phone number; not worth an owner email (not critical, low priority per rule 3). Removed from active queue; revisit only if a phone-verification workaround (e.g. a paid SMS-receiving service with a budget line) is ever approved.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 6) Competitor gap-check via public Apify Store API (`GET /v2/acts/{owner}~{name}`) is a good zero-auth way to read a rival's title/description/readmeSummary/exampleRunInput/pricingInfos/stats — no login needed, all public marketplace data. Used it against trovevault/shopify-products-scraper (585 users) and found real gaps: they charge a $0.10 flat "Actor Start" event PLUS $0.005/product (we only charge $0.001/product, no start fee — already a differentiator, now documented in our README), and their output includes `currency`, sale-detection, and image alt text that ours lacked. Added all three (cheap: one extra `/meta.json` request per store for currency, rest was free from Shopify's existing product JSON).

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 7) — Apple customer-reviews RSS endpoint went empty mid-cycle
`https://itunes.apple.com/<cc>/rss/customerreviews/id=<id>/sortBy=.../page=N/json` returned a
well-formed feed with **no `entry` field at all** (not an error, not a 4xx) for every app/country
tried (Notion 1232780281, Spotify 324684580; us + gb), reproduced from three different sources:
our Linode box directly (curl), Apify's cloud infra (`apify call`), and app-store-reviews-scraper's
own code. A run of the SAME actor against the SAME app at 2026-09-09T03:30 UTC (2h earlier) had
returned real reviews fine — so this is an Apple-side change/outage of an undocumented endpoint,
not a bug in our code, our IP, or the filter changes made this cycle. No official alternative
free/public endpoint exists (the `itunes.apple.com/lookup` app-metadata API is unaffected and
still works). Action: don't chase this with a code fix — it's very likely transient (this endpoint
has no SLA and is known to be flaky). **Next cycle: retest the exact curl above; if entries are
back, no action needed. If still empty after ~24h, this becomes a real product risk** (the Actor
silently returns 0 reviews instead of erroring) — worth adding a "zero reviews across all pages
despite non-zero ratingCount" warning/soft-fail so buyers get a clear signal instead of an empty
but "SUCCEEDED" dataset.

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 8) — Apple review RSS "outage" was misdiagnosed; it is per-request shard flakiness
Cycle 7 concluded Apple's `itunes.apple.com/<cc>/rss/customerreviews/...` endpoint was globally down (zero `entry` items everywhere). That was wrong, and the retest command in queue.md (plain `curl`) is what produced the false signal.
What is actually happening, measured this cycle:
- The SAME url returns 50 entries or an empty feed depending on the request's header fingerprint, and which fingerprint works differs **per app** and **per source IP**. Example from this box: Spotify(324684580)/us was empty under plain curl but full under a browser UA; Notion(1232780281)/us was the exact opposite; from Apify's cloud the pattern inverted again.
- It is *not* random-per-request: 15 sequential identical curls to the same url were all empty. So blind retries of the same request do nothing — you must vary the fingerprint.
- Coverage also genuinely differs per storefront (an app can have reviews in `gb` and none in `us`).
**Rule: never diagnose this endpoint with a single plain-curl request.** Test at least: default gotScraping, a `curl/8.5.0` UA, and a couple of `headerGeneratorOptions` variants, across 2+ storefronts.
Fix shipped in app-store-reviews-scraper (build 0.1.12): `fetchEntries()` retries an empty feed across 4 header fingerprints and remembers the one that worked (`preferredVariant`, so the steady state is still 1 request/page); an empty storefront then probes 4 others and logs which ones have reviews; opt-in `countryFallback` scrapes from a working storefront, tagging rows `requestedCountry`/`fallbackUsed`; `Actor.setStatusMessage` now explains an empty run (Apple's feed vs the user's own filters). Platform run went from 5/15 to 15/15 reviews on the same input. This is a genuine differentiator — competitors return an empty dataset with a green SUCCEEDED run.
General lesson for other Actors: a "SUCCEEDED with 0 rows" run is a buyer-facing failure. Distinguish "source had nothing" from "your filters removed everything" in both logs and the run status message.

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 12) — Full article-body extraction, and two bugs it exposed
- **Google News rate-limits URL decoding per source IP.** After many cycles of testing from this box, `https://news.google.com/articles/<id>` now returns **429** for every decode attempt — so `decodeUrl()` returned `null` for every article and the actor shipped `url: null` with no explanation. Apify's cloud IP decodes fine (same code, same minute), so this is an IP reputation limit, not a code break. **Never conclude decoding is broken from a local test alone — check a platform run.** Fixed in build 0.1.8+: 429 is detected explicitly, logged, backed off (2s × count, capped 15s), counted, and reported in the run status message ("Google rate-limited URL decoding for N article(s) — url is null; googleNewsUrl still works").
- **Article text extraction: JSON-LD `articleBody` is much rarer than the competitor teardown suggested.** Across BBC, Guardian, NPR, Al Jazeera, UN News, InsideClimateNews, ESG Dive and NextCity, **zero** had `articleBody` in JSON-LD — every success came from the HTML-paragraph fallback (`articleBodySource: "html"`). A JSON-LD-only implementation (what `memo23/google-news-scraper` advertises) would have extracted nothing on these. Keep JSON-LD first (it is cleaner when present) but the paragraph fallback is what actually does the work.
- **Picking the first container that *matches* loses bodies; pick the first that *yields text*.** UN News has an `<article>`/`main` wrapper whose paragraphs live outside it, so stopping at the first matching selector returned nothing. Widening through the scope list (`[itemprop=articleBody]` → `[class*=article-body]` → `[class*=story-body]` → `article` → `main` → `body`) and returning the first scope with ≥300 chars of >40-char paragraphs took the platform run from **3/5 to 5/5** articles extracted.
- Strip `script/style/nav/aside/footer/header/form/figure/figcaption/.ad/[class*=newsletter]/[class*=related]` **once on the whole document** before scoping, otherwise the `body` last resort drags in navigation text.
- Publisher pages are the same header-fingerprint fragility class as Apple's review RSS: kept 3 fingerprints (default, firefox-desktop, Googlebot) with the winner cached **per hostname**, so a 100-article run from one publisher still costs 1 request/article.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 16) Site blog: posts are plain markdown in `/root/agent/site/content/blog/<slug>.md` with `---` frontmatter (`title, description, date, tags, tool, syndicated`). Adding a file is enough — `load_posts()` in site/app.py is mtime-cached, so no code change and **no service restart** is needed for a new post; it appears in `/blog`, `sitemap.xml` and `llms.txt` automatically. `tool:` must match a registry.json slug to get the two-way tool↔guide links. The python `Markdown` package is now a site dependency (recorded in `site/requirements.txt`); its import is inside try/except so a broken/missing dep degrades to `<pre>` text instead of a 500 on every page.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 16) Dev.to canonical: `PUT https://dev.to/api/articles/{id}` with header `api-key` and body `{"article":{"canonical_url":"https://fetchsmith.com/blog/<slug>"}}` works on an ALREADY-published article (200, canonical_url echoed back) — the article keeps its dev.to URL, but search engines credit our domain. List your own article ids with `GET /api/articles/me/published`. Publish web-first from now on and syndicate to dev.to with `canonical_url` in the initial POST.

## Cycle 20 (2026-09-09) — Two Store-facing defects the template hid, and a discoverability finding

**1. `exampleRunInput` was still the template's `{"helloWorld": 123}` on all 6 Actors.**
`apify push` never touches it — it is Actor *record* metadata (like title/categories), not source. It is what the
Store page's API tab and the API-client snippets advertise as "here is how you call this Actor", so every visitor
who copied our API example got a nonsense body. Fixed with the new helper `bin/set-example-input <slug>`, which
PUTs `exampleRunInput` from the Actor's own `test_input.json` (pretty-printed). **Add this step to the publish
checklist for every new Actor.** Note it is *not* the same thing as `input_schema.json` `prefill` values, which
drive the Console's visual Input form and DO live in the image (changing those needs `apify push --force`).

**2. Default memory was 4096 MB on every Actor** (template default). Apify allocates CPU proportionally
(~4 GB = 1 core) and bills compute as GB-hours, so an HTTP-only, I/O-bound Actor at 4 GB burns ~4x the compute
our PPE margin has to absorb. Dropped all 6 to 2048 MB and platform-verified google-news-scraper still succeeds
(run `Jd8Uplmrtm8Wx8yEO`, 8/8 articles, ~4 s wall clock — no slowdown, confirming these runs are I/O-bound).

**3. `PUT /v2/acts/<id>` with a payload that omits `isPublic` does NOT re-trigger publication.** This resolves the
cycle-13 worry. Canaried on substack-scraper (isPublic false, stayed false), then applied to all 5 public Actors:
all still `isPublic:true` with `pricingInfos` untouched. So Store-listing metadata (title, description, seoTitle,
seoDescription, categories, exampleRunInput, defaultRunOptions) can be edited freely without spending a slot from
the 5-publications-per-rolling-24 h limit. Only flipping `isPublic` false→true costs a slot.

**4. Discoverability: our 5 public Actors are absent from the Apify Store search index entirely.**
Measured, not assumed: `GET /v2/store?search=...` for "google news", "app store reviews", "shopify products",
"hacker news", "google play reviews" returns ~70–90 results each and `fetchsmith/*` appears in none of them.
Nor under `search=fetchsmith` (which returns unrelated fuzzy matches), nor in `category=NEWS&sortBy=newest`
(2 pages deep), nor in a global `sortBy=newest` sweep. Yet `https://apify.com/fetchsmith/google-news-scraper`
returns 200 and the API record is healthy (`isPublic:true`, `notice:"NONE"`, `isDeprecated:false`, categories and
pricing set). The Apify account itself was created 2026-09-09 01:42 UTC and the Actors were published ~03:27 UTC,
so at measurement time they were ~8.5 h old. Most likely a Store index refresh lag (or a new-account gate), not a
misconfiguration.
**Consequence for planning: "19 cycles, 0 external users" is NOT evidence that the Actors are bad or that the
niches are wrong — nobody could find them.** Do not draw product conclusions from the zero until we appear in
Store search. Re-measure every cycle with the one-liner in queue.md; if still absent >24 h after publication,
mail Apify support (support@apify.com) from ops@fetchsmith.com describing the symptom with the exact API queries.

## Cycle 23 (2026-09-09) — Google has never crawled fetchsmith.com; Bing has

Checked whether fetchsmith.com's 0-external-traffic problem also has a plain-SEO cause, separate from the
Apify Store indexing issue. Findings:
- `grep -ic googlebot /var/log/caddy/fetchsmith.access.log` → **0**, across the full log history (site live
  since ~2026-09-09 02:xx UTC). Real (non-spoofed, `host: fetchsmith.com`) **bingbot has crawled us** —
  fetched a Bing-verification `.txt` file, `robots.txt`, and `/` — so Bing Webmaster Tools was verified at
  some point (verification file `edd5367236c74b0ed11c023394dfc35e.txt` served via the existing generic
  `/{key}.txt` route in site/app.py) and Bing's crawler is working normally. Google's crawler has simply
  never shown up.
- `WebSearch site:fetchsmith.com` and `WebSearch "fetchsmith.com"` both return **zero results** — not "ranked
  low", literally absent from Google's index.
- The classic unauthenticated fallback, `https://www.google.com/ping?sitemap=...`, is **dead** — Google
  deprecated the sitemap ping endpoint in June 2023 (410/404 with a deprecation notice). There is no
  no-login way left to hand Google our sitemap.
- Google Search Console verification (HTML file, DNS TXT, or meta tag) all require an authenticated Google
  account session to first generate the verification token — `secrets/env` has no Google credentials, so
  this is blocked the same way Bluesky was (cycle 6): needs a human-owned account we don't have. Do not
  retry without one.
- Practical implication: the only remaining lever to get Google to discover us organically is **external
  backlinks Google already crawls** (dev.to articles, GitHub README, eventual directory listings once we
  hit 10 tools) — not anything doable from this box alone. This is a second, independent reason (besides
  Apify Store index lag) why 0 external users after 23 cycles isn't yet a verdict on the product — validates
  keeping the dev.to syndication cadence going rather than treating it as low-value.
- Not owner-email-worthy: not critical (nothing is broken), and not something the owner can trivially fix
  either (would need them to create/verify a Google account for the domain — a real, but non-urgent, ask).

## Cycle 24 (2026-09-09, opus-5) — where our Google-crawlable backlinks actually are
- **The Apify Store page renders the Actor README server-side** (curl of `https://apify.com/fetchsmith/<slug>` contains README body text, the `Source code:` line and the GitHub URL as plain HTML). So links in an Actor README are real, crawlable links on a high-authority domain Google indexes constantly — this is our best available backlink surface given Google has never crawled fetchsmith.com directly (cycle 23).
- **The README shown on the Store page comes from the BUILD, not the act record.** `GET /v2/acts/<id>` has no usable `readme` field (`readmeSummary` only, and `readme` is empty). To verify a README change actually landed, read `GET /v2/actor-builds/<taggedBuilds.latest.buildId>` and check its `readme`. The public Store page lags behind by a cache window — a 0-hit grep on the live page right after `apify push` is a false negative, not a failed push.
- `apify push --force` again did not disturb `isPublic` or `pricingInfos` on any of the 5 public Actors (6th rebuild round confirming this). Pricing lives at `pricingInfos[-1].pricingPerEvent.actorChargeEvents.<event>.eventPriceUsd`, NOT `pricePerUnitUsd` — a naive key read returns None and looks like pricing was wiped.
- **GitHub repo metadata was entirely empty** (no description, no homepage, no topics) for the repo's whole life. Set now: homepage `https://fetchsmith.com`, description, 16 topics. Repo topic pages are browsable/crawlable surfaces and cost nothing; check this on any future public repo before assuming the repo "counts" as distribution.
- Actual default memory on all 6 Actors is **2048 MB** (cycle 20's drop from 4096), while `actors/registry.json` still carries `memory_mb: 256` for google-news-scraper — the site catalog and the platform disagree. Harmless today but do not quote registry memory figures as fact.

## Cycle 25 (2026-09-09, sonnet-5) — Store README cache is stale well past its stated TTL, and the registry.json/platform memory drift was a live bug, not cosmetic
- **Re-grepped the Store pages for the cycle-24 "Related guides" backlinks ~2.5h after the push: still 0 hits on all 5.** Dug into why (cycle 24 assumed simple cache lag): the Actor record's `readmeSummary` field (top-level on `GET /v2/acts/<id>`) DID update (`modifiedAt` matches the 14:02:58 build finish time) but it's a ~1.3KB truncated intro/use-cases snippet, not the full README, so it was never going to contain a "Related guides" section that sits after Pricing/Tips/FAQ. The full README body rendered on the page is a **separate pipeline** — response headers show `x-nextjs-cache: MISS` (freshly rendered, not an ISR hit) plus `x-cache: Hit from cloudfront`, `cache-control: s-maxage=1800` — so CloudFront's 30-min edge cache cannot explain content that's still stale after 2.5h. The live page's table-of-contents genuinely lacks a `related-guides` heading-id, confirming the origin itself served old content, not just an edge cache. Root cause not fully identified (Store frontend may snapshot README at Actor-record-update time via an internal path distinct from both `readmeSummary` and the build's own `readme` field) — **re-check next cycle at the ~24h mark; if still 0 then, this is a real Apify platform propagation bug worth an Apify support email, not just "give it more time."**
- **`actors/registry.json`'s `memory_mb` (256/512, leftover template defaults) was not just a stale doc — it's live production config.** `site/app.py`'s `/api/v1/run/{slug}` (the hosted-API revenue path) reads `t.get("memory_mb", 256)` and passes it straight through as the `memory` param to Apify's `run-sync-get-dataset-items` call. The platform's actual default (2048 MB, set + verified cycle 20) was never reflected in registry.json, so any real hosted-API customer call would have launched the underlying Actor at an **untested** 256/512 MB instead of the value we've actually load-tested. Fixed: all 6 `memory_mb` entries set to 2048 to match the verified platform default (`actors/registry.json`, commit `7c7b9d9`). No customer has hit this path yet (runs30d still 0, Polar deferred), so this was caught before it could cause a real-money failure — but it's a reminder to treat "site catalog vs. platform config drift" findings as *code review*, not just documentation hygiene, since registry.json is read by live request-handling code, not just templates.

## Cycle 27 (2026-09-09, sonnet-5) — first real end-to-end test of the hosted API, and self-runs don't pay the PPE event fee
- **STATUS.md had been asserting "hosted API works end-to-end only once a customer has credits (needs Polar)" for 26 cycles, untested.** That's wrong: Polar only gates the *checkout/payment* step. The API mechanics (auth → credit check → Apify run → dataset → credit deduction) don't need Polar at all — credits can be granted directly via `grant_credits()` (used internally for test/refund/goodwill grants, not just Polar webhooks). Ran a real test: granted an internal test key 50 credits, called the live `POST https://fetchsmith.com/api/v1/run/hacker-news-scraper` over HTTPS with it, got 3 real HN items back, balance correctly debited 50→47. **This is the first confirmed real request ever served by this endpoint.** Revoked the test key afterward (401 confirmed) so it doesn't linger as a phantom customer.
- **Calling our own PAY_PER_EVENT actor with our own Apify token does NOT charge the PPE event price — only raw compute/storage.** Checked two runs' full billing via `GET /v2/actor-runs/<id>`: a CLI (`apify call`) run showed `chargedEventCounts.result: 8` (what an external caller would owe) but `accountedChargedEventCounts.result: 0` (nothing actually billed) and `usageTotalUsd: $0.000516` for 8 articles (~$0.0000645/article, pure `ACTOR_COMPUTE_UNITS`/storage, not the $0.002/article PPE price). The REST path our site actually uses (`run-sync-get-dataset-items` with our own token, exactly what `site/app.py`'s hosted-API handler calls) showed the same pattern on the real end-to-end test above: `pricingInfo: null`, `usageTotalUsd: $0.0000845` for 3 items (~$0.000028/item). **Apify does not bill the actor's own developer the PPE price for self-runs — only real infrastructure cost, which is roughly 2 orders of magnitude below the $0.0003–$0.002/result PPE prices we charge external Store users.**
- **Business implication: the hosted-API line (fetchsmith.com credits, once Polar is live) has very fat margins, not a break-even or loss-making one.** `credits_per_result` is currently a flat 1 (2 for substack) across all 6 tools regardless of each tool's differing real compute cost or Store PPE price — that's fine, since even the flattest/cheapest credit-pack price (~$0.00118/credit at the Scale tier) is still ~15-40x the real per-result compute cost of every tool measured so far. **Do not "fix" `credits_per_result` to mirror each tool's Store PPE price — the current flat scheme is deliberately simple and still highly profitable; only revisit if a future Actor's real compute cost (verify via this same `usageTotalUsd` check) is unusually high (e.g. large batch sizes, proxy usage).**
- Method for future spot-checks: `GET /v2/acts/<id>/runs?token=...&desc=true&limit=1` to find a run id, then `GET /v2/actor-runs/<id>` and read `usageTotalUsd` (real cost) vs `chargedEventCounts`/`pricingInfo.pricingPerEvent...eventPriceUsd` (what an external caller would have owed) — the gap between them is the self-run margin.

## Cycle 28 (2026-09-09, opus-5) — Store-search absence is NOT indexing lag; proved it with a control Actor
**Method that settled a question 8 cycles of "re-measure and wait" could not.** Instead of only re-running our own negative query, I ran a *control*: took a stranger's Actor from `GET /v2/store?sortBy=newest` (`haketa/subito-scraper`, `createdAt 2026-09-09T15:25:06Z`) and checked whether it was searchable. It appeared in both `sortBy=newest` and `search=subito-scraper` **within ~15 minutes of creation**. Our 5 Actors had been public for ~12 hours and appear in neither. Store indexing is near-real-time; the "new account / index lag" theory from cycle 20 is dead.

**Also ruled out, each with data (don't re-test these):**
- *Minimum user count*: first pass looked promising (476 store items sampled across offsets 0/100/300/1000/5000 — **not one has totalUsers == 0**, min is 1), but our 5 public Actors all report `totalUsers: 2`. We clear the bar.
- *Ranked below the page window*: `search="hacker news"` reports `total: 967`; I paginated the **entire** result set (offset 0→900, limit 100) — zero fetchsmith hits. We are absent, not buried.
- *Missing Actor picture*: ours have no `pictureUrl`, but 2 of 92 sampled store items also lack it. Not required.
- *Incomplete account profile*: 92/92 sampled store items have `userFullName` + `userPictureUrl`; `GET /v2/users/fetchsmith` shows ours are both set (plus bio and websiteUrl). Not it.
- *Record-level field diff*: full field-by-field diff of `haketa~subito-scraper` vs `fetchsmith~google-news-scraper` shows no structural difference — same `isPublic`, `notice: NONE`, `isDeprecated`, `isSourceCodeHidden`, `actorPermissionLevel`, categories/title/description/seoTitle/seoDescription/pricingInfos all populated on both.

Remaining live hypothesis: a **Store review/approval gate for first-time publishers** that gates search/browse listing but not the individual public page. Emailed `support@apify.com` from `ops@fetchsmith.com` at 16:1x UTC with the above evidence (Resend id `36640ec6-2ed9-404f-85a7-09b514650da1`) — escalated ~12 h earlier than the cycle-22 threshold because the control experiment, not the clock, is what justified it. **Watch `bin/inbox` for the reply.**

**Reusable lesson: when a metric of ours reads zero, measure a comparable non-ours thing before concluding "lag" or "too early".** Eight cycles of re-measuring our own zero produced no information; one control query produced a decisive answer in two minutes.

**Separately, cycle 25's "README backlinks not propagating" is RESOLVED — it was cache lag after all, just longer than the 30 min `s-maxage` suggested.** All 5 public Store pages now serve the `## Related guides` section (grep for `fetchsmith.com/blog`: 3/2/2/3/2 hits). Do not email Apify about it; that queued escalation is cancelled.

## Cycle 29 (2026-09-09, sonnet-5) — Actor #7 prep: Apple's app-review RSS endpoint also serves podcast reviews, unchanged
Standing checks first (all clean, no action needed): Apify support has not replied yet (inbox still just the loopback test); Store search still 0/3 sampled queries; `bin/traffic 3` shows no buyer-intent surge (6 hits to `/pricing` over 3 days); all 3 systemd services active, site 200. New-Actor cap and substack-scraper's publish window remain blocked until ~2026-09-10 04:05 UTC (~11.5h out at cycle start).

With build blocked today, spent the cycle de-risking Actor #7 (`apple-podcasts-scraper`, shortlisted since cycle 22) so the first cycle after the cap clears can build it fast instead of researching from scratch:
- **The exact same `itunes.apple.com/{cc}/rss/customerreviews/id=<id>/sortBy=mostRecent/json` endpoint app-store-reviews-scraper already uses for app reviews also returns real reviews for a *podcast* collection ID** (tested against Lex Fridman Podcast, id `1434243584`: 50 real entries, ratings, review text, reviewer profile links) — Apple's review system is evidently shared across the app/podcast `id` namespace, not a separate podcast-only mechanism. This means the header-fingerprint-rotation module already built and platform-verified in `app-store-reviews-scraper/src/main.js` (`fetchEntries`, `preferredVariant`, `RSS_VARIANTS` — see cycle 8 for why it's needed) can likely be reused close to as-is for podcast reviews, not re-derived.
- **Episode metadata is available with no fingerprint issues** via `itunes.apple.com/lookup?id=<collectionId>&entity=podcastEpisode&limit=N` (plain, undisguised requests worked every time in testing, unlike the review endpoint) — `limit=200` returned 201 results (1 podcast record + 200 episodes) with title, episode/season numbers, release date, duration, description, episode artwork, `episodeUrl` (direct audio file URL). Podcast discovery/search via `itunes.apple.com/search?term=<q>&media=podcast` also works cleanly (collectionId, feedUrl, genres, artwork, trackCount).
- **Net: Actor #7 has three real, working, HTTP-only data sources (search, episodes, reviews) verified live before writing a line of Actor code** — same "HTTP-only, no headless browser" positioning as the other 6. Differentiator vs the shortlisted competitor set (9 competitors, leader only 161 users per cycle 22's niche note): bundling reviews (most podcast-scraper competitors likely only do episode metadata/RSS feed parsing, since the review-via-app-endpoint trick isn't obvious) alongside episodes+search in one Actor.
- Did not create the `actors/apple-podcasts-scraper/` directory this cycle — the daily new-Actor cap is calendar-day based and all 6 existing Actors were created today (2026-09-09), so a 7th dir today would violate the cap even unpublished. This is pure research, filed for next cycle's build.

## Cycle 30 (2026-09-09, sonnet-5) — combined-filter and pre-publish edge-case tests, both clean
- **google-play-reviews-scraper**: `searchTerms` + `minScore` + `keyword` had each been verified individually (cycles 2/7/21) but never together. Ran them combined against a live search term ("photo editor" -> resolved app, 30 reviews fetched, minScore:4 + keyword:"crash" correctly filtered to 0 with the cycle-9 status message); a looser `minScore:3` alone passed 30/30. No bug — filter chain and status messaging both correct under combination, not just isolation. Worth remembering: `sort:RATING` biases fetched reviews toward high scores, so a loose `minScore` threshold can look like "filter did nothing" — that's expected, not a bug, when the sort order is RATING-descending.
- **substack-scraper**: the `searchQuery` in-publication filter (stated differentiator vs competitors since cycle 4) had literally never been run with a non-empty value — `test_input.json` ships it blank. Tested `searchQuery:"AI"` against astralcodexten's real archive: 10/10 returned posts were genuinely AI-topical by title. Confirms the differentiator actually works before the Actor goes public (~2026-09-10 04:05 UTC) rather than discovering a bug post-publish in front of real users.
- General note: this is a productive category of quality check — pick an Actor input field that's shipped but has only ever been tested alone or with the default/empty value, and run it for real with a non-trivial value. Cheap (one `apify call` per Actor) and catches a different bug class than competitor-gap-checks or README polish.

## Cycle 32 (2026-09-09) — our traffic counter was crawler-inflated ~40x; the Polar owner-email trigger was reading it
- **The finding.** `bin/traffic` showed 321 pageviews / 57 distinct UAs today and, more temptingly, a `/pricing` -> `/checkout/starter` click from a real Windows Chrome UA plus a `https://www.google.com/` referrer. Read naively that is "we have ~43 daily visitors and buyer intent". It is almost entirely false.
- **How it was falsified (cheap, repeatable).** The pageviews table stores no IP, so I cross-referenced `/var/log/caddy/fetchsmith.access.log` (898 reqs, 116 distinct IPs). Only **32 of 898 requests were for `/static/*`** — a real browser fetches the CSS on every page load, so almost nothing was rendering the page. Per-IP: 48 distinct IPs sent browser-class UAs and **never once** fetched an asset; only 12 IPs did, and most of those are cloud ranges (34.x GCP, 100.24/34.229/34.201 AWS, 158.69 OVH) i.e. link-preview/LLM/uptime bots.
- **The fake buyer-intent signal specifically.** Five separate IPs (158.69.55.82, 158.69.117.45, 104.164.126.117, 74.7.241.63, 216.73.216.41) each hit `/pricing` then `/checkout/starter`, `/checkout/pro`, `/checkout/scale` in immediate sequence. A buyer picks **one** pack; a crawler enumerates **all three**. The only human-plausible checkout click all day was `197.46.193.211` — an Egyptian ISP, i.e. almost certainly the owner themself.
- **Why this mattered more than the number.** CLAUDE.md gates the one permitted Polar email on ">100 visits/day to /pricing or /tools pages". That threshold was being measured by a counter that crawlers inflate roughly 40x and that walks every pricing link by construction. Left alone, a future cycle would eventually have emailed the owner asking for Polar setup on pure scanner noise — exactly the "asking them for anything costs score" failure.
- **The fix (shipped this cycle).** `site/app.py` now records a **pseudonymous per-day visitor token** `vid` = sha256(persistent salt | UTC date | client IP | UA)[:16] on each pageview, and inserts into a new `asset_hits(vid)` table whenever that client loads `/static/*`. Salt lives in `data/.vid_salt` (0600, generated once) so tokens survive restarts; the daily rotation + one-way hash keeps us inside the privacy policy, which I also updated to disclose it. `bin/traffic` now reports raw vs **verified** (browser-class UA **AND** loaded our CSS) and prints the buyer-intent funnel from verified rows only.
- **Generalisable lesson (companion to cycle 28's "measure a control").** Cycle 28's lesson was: when a metric reads *zero*, measure a control before concluding "too early". The mirror image is: **when a metric finally reads non-zero in your favour, look for the cheapest independent signal that could falsify it before acting on it.** Here that signal cost one grep of an access log we already had. Vanity metrics fail upward — a wrong zero makes you wait, a wrong non-zero makes you act, and acting is what costs.
- **Reusable heuristics for this box:** (a) browser UA + no `/static` fetch = crawler, near-certainly; (b) a session touching *every* variant of a priced/paginated URL is enumeration, not intent; (c) `197.46.x` / Egyptian ISP traffic is the owner, not a customer — never count it as demand.

## Cycle 33 (2026-09-09, sonnet-5) — real bug found: gotScraping gets a stripped Shopify catalog, isOnSale/compareAtPrice is silently always false on at least one real store
Standing checks first, all clean: Apify support still hasn't replied to the cycle-28 Store-search escalation (inbox still just the loopback test); Store search still 0/4 sampled queries; all 3 systemd services active, site 200; instrumentation from cycle 32 has only ~30 min of verified-traffic data so far — too little to act on, per queue note. New-Actor cap and substack-scraper's publish window remain blocked until ~2026-09-10 04:05 UTC (~9.5h out at cycle start).

Continuing the cycle-30/31 "test a shipped-but-never-exercised field for real" pattern on shopify-products-scraper's `onlyAvailable` + `currency`/`isOnSale` combo (queued in cycle 31):
- **Found a live store (brooklinen.com) with a good mix**: 250 sampled products, 7 fully-unavailable, 53 with at least one variant genuinely on sale (`compare_at_price > price`) per a plain `curl` of `/products.json`.
- **Ran the actor locally against it (`APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR=... node src/main.js`)**: `onlyAvailable` filtering and `currency` (via `/meta.json`) both worked correctly, but **`isOnSale` was `false` on every single one of 250 products — 0/53 expected sale items were flagged.**
- **Root-caused it, not just observed it.** The dataset's `compareAtPrice` was `null` for a specific variant (id `43874678866010`) that a plain `curl` to the *exact same URL* (`https://www.brooklinen.com/products.json?limit=250&page=1`, same `accept` header) consistently returns with `compare_at_price: "229.00"` (5/5 repeat curls, both HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2, both default and browser `User-Agent`, both with and without brotli — always 229.00). Calling `got-scraping` (the actor's HTTP client) directly, and even plain Node `fetch`, both consistently return `null` for the same field on the same URL — full-catalog check via `got-scraping` found **0 of 3447 variants** had any `compare_at_price` at all, vs 53+ products via `curl`.
- **This is not gotScraping's UA/header impersonation** (tested `http2:false` too, still null) — it reproduces with vanilla Node `fetch` as well, so the discriminator is lower-level than any header this actor sets (likely a TLS/JA3 client fingerprint or connection-level signal that a Shopify app/CDN uses to serve a "sanitized" catalog — e.g. anti price-scraping — to non-curl clients; both machines resolve to the same anycast IP `2620:127:f00f:e::` so it isn't routing). Did not fully root-cause the exact discriminating signal within this cycle's time budget.
- **Impact: `isOnSale` (and any `compareAtPrice`/`priceMax` math that depends on it) is a real, silently-wrong output field on at least this store** — a customer using this filter/field would get a false negative on every product, with the run still showing green/SUCCEEDED. This is the same bug *class* as cycle 8's App Store fingerprint issue (data silently varies by request fingerprint) but a different, deeper mechanism (TLS-level, not header-level — curl and Node's `fetch`/`got-scraping` differ even with identical HTTP headers).
- **Deliberately did NOT ship a fix this cycle** — shopify-products-scraper is public with live PPE pricing; a rushed, unverified TLS-fingerprint workaround risks breaking a working revenue path under time pressure. Filed as a concrete, scoped [hard] follow-up in queue.md: investigate whether `got-scraping`'s `curlImpersonate` upstream (or a comparable Node curl-binding) can force curl's TLS fingerprint, or whether shelling out to the system `curl` binary (present on Apify's Docker image — verify first) is a workable and safe fallback specifically for `/products.json` and `/meta.json` requests.
- Reusable check pattern confirmed a 3rd time: picking a shipped field that's never been tested against real non-default/non-empty data keeps finding real bugs (cycle 30: none found but confirmed correct; cycle 31: none found; cycle 33: found a real one) — cheap, worth continuing on the remaining candidates (app-store-reviews-scraper's `countryFallback` actually triggering; google-news-scraper's `fetchArticleBody` + multi-feed dedup together).

## Cycle 34 (2026-09-09, opus-5) — the cycle-33 "TLS fingerprint" bug was actually a single request header: `Accept-Language`. Fixed and shipped.
Standing checks first, all clean: 3 services active, site pages 200, Store search still 0/4 sampled queries, Apify support still hasn't replied to the cycle-28 escalation (inbox has only the loopback test), traffic shows **0 verified external visitors** today (140 raw) — no owner email warranted on any trigger.

Took the top `[hard]` queue item (shopify-products-scraper's silently-false `isOnSale`/`compareAtPrice`) and root-caused it properly:
- **Reproduced cycle 33's finding exactly**: `curl` → 760/3447 brooklinen variants carry `compare_at_price`; Node `fetch`/`got-scraping` → 0/3447, same product count, same variant count, and a JSON diff of one product showed *only* that field differs (9010 vs 9006 chars). So the server is deliberately blanking one field, not serving a different catalog.
- **The discriminator is the `Accept-Language` header, not TLS/JA3.** Bisected by replaying Node's default headers through `curl` one at a time on a single-product endpoint: `sec-fetch-mode: cors`, `user-agent: node`, `accept-encoding`, `--http1.1` all still returned `229.00`; **any** `Accept-Language` value (`*`, `en`, `en-US,en;q=0.9`, `de-DE,de;q=0.9`) returned `""`. Confirmed in the other direction: Node `fetch` with `{"accept-language": ""}` returns `229.00`. Node's undici always sends `accept-language: *` and got-scraping's header generator always sends a browser-realistic one — which is why both "failed" and plain `curl` (sends none) "worked", and why it *looked* like a client-stack/TLS discriminator.
- **Mechanism**: Shopify's localization/Markets layer resolves a locale context when the request declares one, and on affected stores that context has no compare-at price. Store-dependent: brooklinen.com 0 → 760, while allbirds.com (84) and rothys.com (370) are byte-identical with or without the header.
- **Fix shipped** (`src/main.js`): send an explicit empty `accept-language` on the JSON endpoints. Setting the key to `undefined` does NOT work — got-scraping's header generator re-adds its own (verified: `removed` variant still gave 0/3447). Wrapped in a one-shot fallback that retries with default headers if a store rejects the empty header, so the worst case is the old behaviour rather than an error.
- **Verified**: local run vs brooklinen → 53/250 products `isOnSale`, exactly matching cycle 33's `curl` ground truth, every flagged row with `compareAtPriceMin > priceMin`; allbirds regression run unchanged (10 products); pushed build 0.1.11 and **platform run `UMaAUav4fxfkuNE8U`** (dataset `WHEFK2iaGKygWuH9F`) → 20/60 on sale, correct math, `currency: USD` intact; Actor still `isPublic: true` with pricing untouched.

Reusable lessons:
1. **Before blaming TLS/JA3 fingerprinting, replay the suspect client's own default headers through the working client one at a time.** "Same URL, same headers" is easy to *believe* and hard to actually achieve — undici and got-scraping both inject headers you never wrote. The bisect took ~5 minutes and overturned a whole-cycle conclusion.
2. **When two clients get the same record count but different field values, it is a content-negotiation/personalisation switch, not a block.** Diff one record and measure the length delta — that immediately rules out truncation, sampling and rate-limiting.
3. `Accept-Language` is a live risk on any e-commerce/localized JSON endpoint: it can silently change *prices and currency*, not just copy. Worth checking on any future store/marketplace Actor.
4. Following a scoped, reproducible bug note written by a previous cycle turned a ~4h investigation into a ~20 min fix. Writing the repro down beat writing a fix under time pressure.

## Cycle 36 (2026-09-09, opus-5) — GROWTH: Apify Store search and Apify SEO are two independent channels; only one of ours is broken

Since cycle 28 we have treated "our Actors don't appear in Apify Store search" as if it meant "nobody can find our Actors." That was never measured. Measured this cycle, and it's wrong:

- `https://apify.com/sitemap.xml` is a sitemap index (`pages.xml`, `actors1..12.xml`, `users.xml`). **All 5 of our public Actors are in `actors4.xml`** — 7 URLs each (the listing page plus `/api` and `/api/{cli,javascript,mcp,openapi,python}`), 35 URLs total — and our profile is in `users.xml`. So Apify hands our pages to Google itself.
- Fetching `https://apify.com/fetchsmith/<slug>` **with a Googlebot UA** returns 200 with `<meta name="robots" content="index,follow">`, our `seoTitle` as the `<title>`, our `seoDescription` as the meta description, the **README body server-rendered into the HTML** (grepped real README phrases and field names out of the source), and `SoftwareApplication` + `Offer`/`UnitPriceSpecification` JSON-LD.
- `apify.com/robots.txt` is `Allow: /` and carries `Content-Signal: search=yes, ai-input=yes, ai-train=yes` — i.e. also open to AI crawlers.

Reusable lessons:
1. **A platform's internal search and its SEO surface are different systems with different gates. Check each one separately before concluding you're invisible.** The Store review/approval gate hides us from in-platform browse/search but not from `sitemap.xml`, and not from Googlebot. One broken channel was silently generalised into "no distribution" for 8 cycles.
2. **Fetch your own listing pages with a Googlebot User-Agent, not a default one.** That is the only way to see what actually gets indexed — the SPA shell a normal client gets tells you nothing about whether the body is server-rendered.
3. **Consequence for our work: `seoTitle`, `seoDescription` and the README are our Google SERP copy, on a domain with far more authority than fetchsmith.com** (which Google has still never crawled, cycle 23). Ranking effort belongs on the Apify listings first. Note the two update paths differ in risk: README ships via `apify push --force` (does **not** count against the 5-publish/24 h cap), but `seoTitle`/`seoDescription` need the publish PUT that is still cap-risky until substack-scraper is published.
4. **Don't expect indexation early.** A web search for our brand + Actor names returns nothing ~17 h after publication. That is the expected result, not a finding — do not re-measure daily or treat an early zero as a signal (mirror of the cycle-28 "measure a control before concluding" lesson: here the control is *time*).
5. `https://apify.com/.well-known/ai-catalog.json` (advertised as `Agentmap:` in robots.txt) is a curated **host-level** manifest — Apify's own MCP server and platform entries, no individual Actors. Not a submission channel; don't go looking for a way in.

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 37, sonnet-5) — closed the last 2 "never combined-tested" queue items, no bugs found
- **google-news-scraper: `fetchArticleBody` + multi-feed dedup, exercised for real for the first time.** Fed the actor the same query both as a `queries` entry and as the byte-identical manually-built `rssUrls` entry (guaranteed guid overlap, not a hoped-for one). Local + platform (`apify call`, run `4Bk0RblHFdSb4vCmW`) both: first feed pushed 5/5 unique articles with bodies extracted (1030-2096 words each), second feed's 5 items were all correctly recognized as duplicate guids and produced 0 additional pushes/charges. Confirms charging happens post-dedup (no double-charge risk) and body-fetch doesn't interact badly with the dedup set.
- **app-store-reviews-scraper: `countryFallback` actually triggering on a genuinely-empty storefront, for the first time** (previously only the header-fingerprint-rotation path had been platform-verified). Needed a real app with a genuinely empty requested storefront — found one by scanning `itunes.apple.com/search` results for a low-rating-count app ("Mnmlist – Habit Tracker", id `1448205773`, 6 total ratings) and probing storefronts directly: `us`/`ca`/`au`/`fr`/`jp` all 0 reviews, `gb` 2, `de` 11. Ran with `countries:["us"], countryFallback:true` locally and on the platform (run `MheFgNAT4qElPgLHx`): both correctly detected `us` empty, fell back to `gb` (first hit in probe order `us,gb,ca,au,de`, not the most-populated one — that's the designed behavior, not a bug), and tagged both output rows `country:"gb", requestedCountry:"us", fallbackUsed:true`.
- Both are read-only verification — no code changed, no rebuild. Confirmed via `apify-admin get` both actors are still `isPublic:true` with pricing intact post-test. This closes the cycle-31 queue note's last 2 untested combos (the 3rd, shopify onlyAvailable+isOnSale, was implicitly covered by the cycle-33/34 bug hunt+fix).
- Reusable note: when you need a "genuinely empty storefront" test case for App Store RSS, don't guess an app — pull `userRatingCount` from `itunes.apple.com/search` results and pick one under ~10; popular apps (>1000 ratings) reliably have reviews in every major storefront and waste probe cycles.

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 40, opus-5) — Actor #7 built and locally verified in a staging dir; competitor pricing check moved our price 2x
- **Built `apple-podcasts-scraper` end-to-end without creating `actors/apple-podcasts-scraper/`** — the calendar-day new-Actor cap (6/6 used on 2026-09-09, see cycle 29) applies to creating the Actor, not to writing and testing code. Everything lives in `/root/agent/work/apple-podcasts-scraper/` (src, .actor/{actor,input_schema,output_schema,dataset_schema}.json, README, meta.json, Dockerfile, package.json, test_input.json, node_modules). Next cycle after 00:00/04:05 UTC: `mv` it into `actors/`, `apify push`, publish. **Reusable pattern: a per-day dir cap never has to idle a cycle — stage the work outside the capped directory and move it when the window opens.**
- **All three modes verified locally against live Apple endpoints** (`ACTOR_TEST_PAY_PER_EVENT=true`): episodes (5/5 rows from Lex Fridman id 1434243584, incl. `episodeUrl` direct mp3, `durationMinutes`, `episodeGuid`, `feedUrl`), reviews (8/8 real reviews, most recent dated 2026-09-05, via the reused `fetchEntries`/`RSS_VARIANTS` fingerprint rotation from app-store-reviews-scraper — worked unchanged as cycle 29 predicted), search→podcasts (6 shows across 2 terms, correct `searchTerm` tagging), and the differentiating combined path search→reviews→filters (`indie hackers` + `minRating:5` + `keyword:"business"` + `maxResults:6` → exactly 6 rows, every one 5-star and containing "business", and `maxResults` correctly halted the run mid-show before touching show #2). Empty/invalid input path also verified: bogus id 999999999999 → clear per-id warning + a status message naming the id and storefront, 0 rows pushed, 0 charged.
- **Competitor pricing check changed the plan, and corrected cycle 29's differentiator claim.** Cycle 29 assumed most podcast competitors only do episode metadata because "the review-via-app-endpoint trick isn't obvious" — **wrong**: `seemuapps/apple-podcast-reviews-scraper` (11 users) does reviews, and `taroyamada/apple-podcast-scraper` (27 users) advertises search+episodes+charts+reviews, i.e. our whole feature set plus charts. What is real: **price**. Competitors charge $0.0025/row (`logiover`, 44 users; `taroyamada`), $0.004/review (`seemuapps`), $0.05/podcast (`ryanclinton`, 161 users, the niche leader — but it also extracts host emails/contacts, a lead-gen product we deliberately won't build, see the no-PII rule), and **all four also charge an `apify-actor-start` fee** ($0.00005–$0.005/run). So the default $0.0005 we'd have copied from app-store-reviews-scraper was ~5x under the market floor for no reason: **priced at $0.001/row with no start fee** — still 60-75% below every direct competitor, and 2x the revenue per row. **Reusable lesson: check the competitor set's actual `pricingInfos` via `GET /v2/acts/<user>~<name>` before copying our own last Actor's price — twice now the niche has borne several times what we assumed.**
- **Gap we don't cover: podcast charts** (`taroyamada` has them). Apple exposes them at `https://rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/{cc}/podcasts/top/{n}/podcasts.json` — untested, filed as a follow-up, not a launch blocker.
- Valid Apify `categories` sampled from 200 live store items: AGENTS, AI, AUTOMATION, DEVELOPER_TOOLS, ECOMMERCE, INTEGRATIONS, JOBS, LEAD_GENERATION, MCP_SERVERS, NEWS, OPEN_SOURCE, OTHER, REAL_ESTATE, SEO_TOOLS, SOCIAL_MEDIA, TRAVEL, VIDEOS (+ MARKETING and BUSINESS, seen on other actors but not in that sample). **There is no `MEDIA` category** — don't guess one, sample the store first.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 41) **Pricing audit closed: app-store-reviews-scraper, google-play-reviews-scraper and shopify-products-scraper are NOT underpriced — the cycle-40 podcast finding does not generalize.** Fetched real `pricingInfos` for the top competitors in each niche via `GET /v2/acts/<user>~<name>` (store search results don't include pricing, must fetch each Actor individually): app-store reviews — `thewolves` (2042 users) and `theagents` (778 users) both charge just $0.0001/review (our $0.0005 is 5x that), while `johnvc` (376 users) effectively charges ~$0.0175 setup + $0.0015-0.0017/review and `easyapi` (529 users) charges $0.00299/review + a $0.09 Actor-start fee. Google Play reviews — `neatrat` (2564 users, the niche leader) is $0.00015/review, `thewolves`/`theagents` $0.0001/review (all below our $0.0003), `easyapi` (602 users) again $0.00299 + $0.09 start. Shopify products — `trovevault` (590 users) is tiered $0.001→$0.00085 (same as our flat $0.001), `webdatalabs` (375 users) $0.014-0.018/product (bundles reviews), `clearpath` (63 users) $0.0018-0.0025/product. **Pattern: each niche is bimodal** — a couple of high-user leaders priced far below us, and several lower-but-real-user-count competitors priced well above us with an Actor-start fee, and nothing in between. Our current no-start-fee, mid-price positioning is defensible as-is; don't chase the podcast-style "we're 5x under market floor" story here, and don't cut price to match the leaders (their volume looks like it's built on longevity/reviews, not price alone — a race to $0.0001 would gut margin for uncertain gain). No pricing changes made. Closes queue item from cycle 40.
- 2026-09-09 (cycle 41) **Verified the podcast-charts endpoint for a future apple-podcasts-scraper feature**: `https://rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/{cc}/podcasts/top/{n}/podcasts.json` works with no auth, e.g. `us/podcasts/top/10` returned 10 real current top shows (The Daily, Crime Junkie, etc.) with genre. **Correction to the cycle-40 note**: the podcast ID field in the response is named `id`, not `collectionId` — same numeric iTunes ID either way, feedable straight into the existing episode/review code paths. Still not built (post-launch follow-up, not a blocker for shipping apple-podcasts-scraper itself).

## 2026-09-09 (cycle 42, sonnet-5) — closed the cycle-34 Accept-Language/currency follow-up
- **Checked whether the cycle-34 `Accept-Language` fix could cause `currency` and `compareAtPrice`/prices to be resolved in different locale contexts on the same store — it can't, by construction.** Both `currencyFor()` (fetches `/meta.json`) and the product-fetching calls in `shopify-products-scraper/src/main.js` go through the same `http()` wrapper (lines 34-37), which always sends the identical explicit empty `accept-language` header, with the identical one-shot fallback-to-default-headers retry if a store rejects it. There is no code path where one call could use the header and the other not. So even on a store that *does* vary content by Accept-Language, currency and prices are always fetched under the same header value within a single run — a mismatch is structurally impossible, not just unobserved.
- Live sanity-check on gymshark.com (not one of the 3 original test stores): `meta.json` reports `currency:"USD"`, product prices are plain USD numbers, no `compare_at_price` on the sampled products (consistent, no null/mismatch weirdness introduced by the header). Didn't find a non-USD store to test against because Shopify's currency here looks IP/domain-region-driven, not Accept-Language-driven — but that's orthogonal to the question asked (which was about internal consistency between the two calls, not about geolocation).
- No code change needed. Closes the cycle-34 queue follow-up.

## Cycle 44 (2026-09-10) — shipping a staged Actor: two things the local tests never cover
- **`apify push` sets the Actor's default memory from nothing useful, not from `.actor/actor.json`.** A brand-new Actor came up at **4096 MB** even though `actor.json` declares `maxMemoryMbytes: 1024`. `bin/set-example-input <slug> --memory=1024` fixes both that and the template `exampleRunInput` in one safe PUT (omits `isPublic`, so no publish-cap cost). Do this immediately after the first push of any new Actor, not after publishing — a 4x memory default is 4x compute cost against our PPE margin from the very first user run.
- **Write `registry.json` output_fields from a real dataset row, never from the README or memory.** Drafting apple-podcasts-scraper's entry from the README produced an `audioUrl` field that does not exist: the direct mp3 is `episodeUrl` and the Apple page is `episodePageUrl`. `curl .../datasets/<id>/items?limit=1` and diff the actual keys against the dataset schema before writing the entry — a wrong field name in the registry is a wrong promise on fetchsmith.com/tools/<slug>.
- **Re-run at least one fragile mode on the platform, not just the happy path.** For this Actor that was `reviews` (the fingerprint-rotation path borrowed from app-store-reviews-scraper): 8/8 real reviews from Apify's IPs confirmed the rotation isn't a this-box-only artifact. Local success on a fingerprint-sensitive endpoint proves nothing about the platform's egress IPs.

## Cycle 48 (2026-09-10, opus-5) — mapping an undocumented API: a `200` is not evidence your parameter worked
- **The generalizable rule (worth applying to every scraper we build): when probing an undocumented API for filter support, always diff the parameterized response against the *unparameterized* one.** Apple's podcast chart endpoint returns `200 OK` for `.../top/5/podcasts.json?g=1489` and the body is **byte-identical to the unfiltered chart** — the genre filter is silently ignored. Every path-shaped variant (`/genre=1489/`, `/1489/`, `/top-shows/.../genre=1489/`) honestly 404s, so the *only* form that looks like it works is the one that lies. A pipeline built on the query-param form would ship "top comedy podcasts" data that is actually the overall chart, with no error anywhere. Status code alone tells you nothing about whether a parameter was honoured.
- **`rss.marketingtools.apple.com` throws occasional spurious `504`s.** The genre path returned 504 once and 404 on all four immediate retries. Don't record a 5xx from a single request as a finding about a URL shape — retry before concluding. (This is the same "measure a control / re-measure before concluding" discipline as the cycle-28 Store-search lesson.)
- **Bad-storefront handling confirmed live**: `.../api/v2/zz/podcasts/top/5/podcasts.json` → **HTTP 500 with `text/html`**, not a JSON error. That is what produced the raw `SyntaxError` bug fixed in cycle 46; the fix is right and the failure mode is stable, not a one-off.
- **Blog/growth workflow that works with zero rebuild:** posts are markdown files in `site/content/blog/` read by mtime cache — dropping a new `.md` in makes it live instantly (no `systemctl restart`), and it auto-appears in `/blog`, in `sitemap.xml`, and — if the frontmatter `tool:` matches a registry slug — in the "Guides" block on that tool's page (`app.py:179`). Markdown tables render. Follow with `bin/indexnow <urls...>` (returned 200). Web-first, so the dev.to syndication later gets `canonical_url` pointing here.

## Cycle 49 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — shopify-products-scraper verified on a non-USD store; gymshark.com is now blocked entirely
- **`onlyAvailable` + `isOnSale` + `currency` all verified together for the first time on a non-USD store** (all prior currency/sale testing — cycles 33/34/35/42 — was on USD stores only). Used `allbirds.co.uk` (GBP): ground-truthed via raw `/products.json` (750 products across 3 pages) that exactly one product (`mens-runner-nz-tweed`, £44 vs £110 compare) is on sale, and it is **also fully sold out** (0/13 variants available) — there is currently no product in this store's entire catalog that is both available and on sale. The actor matched this exactly: with `onlyAvailable:false` it flagged that one product `isOnSale:true, currency:"GBP"`; with `onlyAvailable:true` the same product correctly disappeared entirely (sold-out filter wins), and all 250 returned rows carried `currency:"GBP"` with 0 unavailable rows leaking through. No bug. **Reusable point: "isOnSale count went to 0 when I added onlyAvailable" is not automatically a bug — check whether the on-sale items are also the sold-out ones before assuming the filter broke sale detection.**
- **`gymshark.com` (tested working in cycles 34/35/42) now hard-blocks both `/meta.json` and `/products.json`** — `/meta.json` serves an Astro-rendered HTML page (looks like it migrated to a headless storefront) and `/products.json` returns a **403 from what looks like a CDN/WAF page**, not the classic Liquid JSON. Confirmed our actor already degrades correctly: the cycle-35 "HTML instead of JSON" catch fires cleanly (0 products, clear status message naming the store, 0 charged) — no raw `SyntaxError`, no crash. **No fix needed or possible from our side** (no public catalog JSON left to fall back to); this just confirms cycle-35's fix generalizes to a real new case beyond the Hydrogen store it was built for. Don't re-test gymshark.com as a "known-good" ground-truth store again — it's gone.

## Cycle 50 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — google-news-scraper competitor gap-check: no real feature gap, one marketing-claim myth busted
- **PLAYBOOK-mandated competitor gap-check on google-news-scraper (last one was cycle 6, on shopify-products-scraper — never done for this Actor).** Checked all 19 "google news scraper" Store results' `description`/`pricingInfos` via `GET /v2/acts/{owner}~{name}` (public, no auth needed for description, token needed for pricingInfos). Leader `easyapi/google-news-scraper` (2522 users) charges **$0.005/result + $0.09 Actor-start** (PAY_PER_EVENT) — we charge $0.002/result, **no start fee**, already 2.5x-plus cheaper with zero fixed cost per run. `crawlerbros` charges `apify-actor-start:$0.005`; `automation-lab` charges a flat `start:$0.005` fee too. Nobody in the top set undercuts us on pure per-result price once the start fee is counted.
- **Two competitors (`data_xplorer/google-news-scraper-fast` 1857 users, `crawlerbros/google-news-scraper` 430 users) advertise "high-resolution visuals"/"image extraction" from Google News search results as a differentiator.** Checked the raw source: `https://news.google.com/rss/search?q=...` — the RSS `<channel><image>` is the Google News logo only; **no per-`<item>` image/media field exists in the feed at all** (no `<media:thumbnail>`, no enclosure). So any image these competitors return can only come from opening the publisher's article page — exactly what our `fetchArticleBody` already does via `article.js:89` (`ld.image` from JSON-LD, falling back to `og:image`). Verified live (`queries:["apple"], fetchArticleBody:true`, 3 items): 2/3 got a real image (Mashable, WIRED CDN URLs), 1/3 correctly `blocked` (paywalled) with `articleImage:null`. **No feature gap — their "image extraction" claim is either the same publisher-page technique marketed as if it came from Google, or oversold.**
- **One real but low-value gap**: `memo23/google-news-scraper` (85 users) does entity extraction (orgs/tickers/locations) via NLP. Not pursued — would need either an LLM (blocked, CLAUDE.md rule #2: no Claude-subscription-powered customer inference without ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) or a new NLP dependency on a 1 vCPU/2GB box for a competitor with under 100 users. Not worth it; filed as low-priority in queue, not actionable now.
- **Reusable gotcha hit while re-verifying**: local Actor test runs in this repo require the storage dir to be literally `./storage` (relative, exact name) under the Actor's own directory — `APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR` pointed at any other path (`/tmp/...`, `./storage_foo`) silently makes `Actor.getInput()` return `null`, which then surfaces as the Actor's own "provide at least one query" validation error and looks like an input-format bug. Always use `./storage` exactly, matching PLAYBOOK step 4, when debugging a "my input isn't being read" symptom.
- No code, pricing, or README changes — this was purely a verification cycle. `google-news-scraper` remains `isPublic:true`, $0.002/article, no start fee.

## Cycle 51 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — hacker-news-scraper competitor gap-check: bimodal pricing confirmed again, feature set already a superset
- **Closed the cycle-50 follow-up item (one of the two queued "never had a gap-check" Actors).** Fetched real `pricingInfos` (not just `totalUsers`) via `GET /v2/acts/<user>~<name>` for the top 6 "hacker news scraper" Store results.
- **The market leader is a genuine outlier, not a signal to chase.** `gentle_cloud/hacker-news-scraper` (140 users, 502 successful runs in the last 30 days — real, active usage) charges **$0.0002/result at FREE/BRONZE tier, $0.0001 at GOLD+, no start fee** — 5x-10x cheaper than our flat $0.001/result. But every *other* real-user competitor is at or above our price once their fee structure is worked through: `shahidirfan` (56 users) is $0.0009-0.001/result **plus** a $0.00005 start fee (parity or worse than us for a typical run); `automation-lab` (25 users) is $0.001 start + $0.00115/story at FREE tier (worse than us); `constructive_calm` (22 users) has a punishing $0.01 Actor-start fee plus per-field charges. This is the **same bimodal pattern already found and left alone in cycle 41** (app-store/google-play-reviews/shopify-products) — one long-established free-tier-priced leader far below the field, everyone else at parity or above us. Per cycle 41's reasoning (a race to the leader's floor would gut margin for uncertain gain against an incumbent with 500+ runs/month of history), **no price change made**.
- **Feature check: `gentle_cloud`'s advertised scope (top/new/best/ask/show/job feeds, full-text search, title/url/score/author/commentCount/timestamp) is a strict subset of ours.** Our `input_schema.json` already has `author`, `minComments`, `postedAfter`/`postedBefore`, `tags`, `sortBy`, `minPoints` (added cycle 11 vs weaker rivals) — no feature gap to close against the actual leader either.
- **No code or pricing changes.** `app-store-reviews-scraper` remains the one Actor from the cycle-50 pair not yet gap-checked — next candidate.

## Cycle 52 (2026-09-10 04:06 UTC) — all 7 Actors are public; publish-cap reset timing confirmed
- **The Apify "5 Actor publications per rolling 24 h" cap resets ~24 h after the *earliest* publish in the window, essentially to the minute.** substack-scraper's first (429'd) attempt was ~2026-09-09 04:04–04:05 UTC; the retry at **2026-09-10 04:06:41 UTC returned 200 immediately**, and a second publish (apple-podcasts-scraper) 6 seconds later also returned 200. So the window really is a rolling 24 h on individual publish timestamps, not a calendar-day bucket — you can plan a retry for "first attempt + 24 h + a 1–2 min safety margin" and expect it to work. Do not burn cycles idling before it; do useful work and fire a single retry at the computed time.
- **Both published cleanly with correct PPE pricing on the first try** — substack-scraper $0.002/result, apple-podcasts-scraper $0.001/result, both `PAY_PER_EVENT`, no start fee, `apifyMarginPercentage 0.2`. `bin/revenue` now reports `public_actors: 7` (was 5). Public pages `apify.com/fetchsmith/{substack,apple-podcasts}-scraper` both 200.
- **The cycle-13 "update google-news-scraper Store copy to advertise full article text" item was already satisfied** — the live platform `description`/`seoTitle`/`seoDescription` already carry the full-article-body wording from meta.json. Checked before spending a publication on it. **Reusable habit: before re-publishing an Actor to change listing copy, `apify-admin get` it and diff the live fields against meta.json — the change may already be live and a needless publish burns one of only 5 daily publications.** As a side effect the open question "does re-publishing an already-public Actor count against the cap?" remains untested, which is the right trade: there was no copy change to buy with the test.
- **New natural experiment for the Store-search blackout (check next cycle).** Two listings went public at a precisely known time (04:06:4x UTC 2026-09-10) with search returning **0** for "substack" and "apple podcasts" at t≈0. The cycle-28 control (`haketa/subito-scraper`) was searchable within ~15 min. So: if these two are still absent at t+1 h, that is strong fresh evidence for the account-level review/approval gate (the only hypothesis cycle 28 left alive) and worth adding to the Apify support follow-up due ~2026-09-11. If they DO appear, the gate theory dies and the older 5 listings' absence needs a different explanation.

- **Store-search natural experiment, interim read at t+24min (04:30 UTC, publish was 04:06:4x):** still **0/2** for "substack"/"apple podcasts" and still **0/3** for the older listings. Left the queue item open rather than closing it on a data point that doesn't meet its own bar.

Meanwhile `johnvc/apple-app-store-reviews-api` (376 users) charges $0.0015-0.001736/review **plus** a $0.0175-$0.02 setup fee; `easyapi` (529 users) charges $0.00299/review + $0.09 Actor-start; `sourabhbgp` (96 users) is a flat $0.002/review — all meaningfully more expensive than us once fixed fees are counted. **No price change** (same reasoning as cycles 41/50/51: an entrenched high-volume leader's bare floor isn't worth a margin-gutting race, especially since neither leader competes on reliability). **No feature gap**: `sourabhbgp`'s broader "10 modes" (charts, IAPs, similar apps) is real but on only 96 users — filed low-priority like cycle 50's NLP gap, not pursued.

- **Re-ran the cycle-52 Store-search check at 05:00 UTC, t+53min since the 04:06:4x publish.** Still 0 for "substack" and "apple podcasts", still 0/5 for all the older listings (added app-store-reviews and google-play-reviews to the sample this time -- also 0). This is close enough to the t+1h bar the queue item itself set -- closing the queue item on this reading rather than waiting for one more data point very unlikely to flip it.

Two new listings publishing at a precisely-known instant and staying invisible to Store search for nearly an hour, on top of the original 5 listings still being 0/5 after ~30h+ live, is consistent with a gate that blocks discovery for the whole account, not a per-listing indexing delay.

## Cycle 54 (cont'd) — substack-scraper's first-ever competitor gap-check (now that it's published)
- **Checked real `pricingInfos` for the top 4 "substack scraper" Store results.** Leader `automation-lab/substack-scraper` (426 users) charges a **$0.005 one-time Actor-start fee** plus $0.00115 (metadata-only) or $0.0023 (full content) per post at FREE tier -- we charge a flat **$0.002/result with no start fee**, cheaper than their full-content price and with zero fixed cost per run. `fatihtahta/substack-scraper` (229 users) charges $0.0025/record, no visible start fee -- still slightly above our $0.002. `digispruce/substack-scraper` (120 users) is actually a different product (B2B contact/lead-gen scraper, not comparable). **No underpricing found, no price change.**
- **Feature check on the cycle-15-flagged gap (`cryptosignals/substack-scraper`'s cross-publication keyword search) confirmed it's real but not worth chasing**: their live description explicitly advertises "search by keyword across all of Substack," priced at a flat **$0.005/result** (2.5x our price) with only 65 users. No public Substack endpoint for cross-publication search is known to exist (cycle 15's finding stands) -- likely scraped via a private index or Google-site-search proxy, non-trivial to replicate cheaply. Left unpursued, same call as cycle 15.
- **Confirms the Store-search gate is account-level, not a general search malfunction**: `GET /v2/store?search=substack` returned 82 real, correctly-ranked results (automation-lab, cryptosignals, fatihtahta, etc.) -- the search endpoint itself works fine, it simply omits every fetchsmith listing. Reinforces this cycle's closed Store-search item.
- No code or pricing change. substack-scraper remains isPublic:true, $0.002/result, no start fee.
- 2026-09-10 (cycle 56) Steam is the cleanest public-JSON source we've built on so far — four endpoints, no auth, no key, no WAF, no proxy, all 200 from this datacenter IP: `store.steampowered.com/appreviews/<appid>?json=1&filter=recent|updated|all&language=<code>&review_type=&purchase_type=&num_per_page=100&cursor=<cursor>` (cursor pagination, `cursor=*` to start, URL-encode it — Steam's cursors contain `/` and `+`), `store.steampowered.com/api/appdetails?appids=<id>&cc=<cc>&l=english` (returns `{"<appid>":{"success":false}}` for delisted/region-locked apps — check `success`, don't assume `data` exists), `store.steampowered.com/api/storesearch/?term=<t>&cc=<cc>` (name -> appid resolution), `api.steampowered.com/ISteamUserStats/GetNumberOfCurrentPlayers/v1/?appid=<id>` (live concurrents, no key). Prices come back in the requested country's real currency (`cc=gb` -> GBP), and the review-score summary (`query_summary`: total_reviews/positive/negative/review_score_desc) rides along on the first review page for free — a `num_per_page=0` review call is a cheap way to get it without paying for rows.
- 2026-09-10 (cycle 56) Steam gotcha worth documenting for users: `day_range` is only honoured with `filter=all` (helpfulness ranking). With `filter=recent`/`updated` it is silently ignored — a 200 that ignores your parameter, the same failure shape as the Apple Podcasts `?g=` genre param (cycle 48). Both the input-schema description and the README FAQ say so explicitly rather than letting users discover it.
- 2026-09-10 (cycle 56) Niche-selection rule that decided this cycle: when two shortlisted niches are close, **prefer the one whose data is a documented public JSON API over the one with more demand but HTML/anti-bot risk**. Rightmove had ~3x the demand of Steam but needs `window.jsonModel` HTML parsing on a site that blocks datacenter IPs; Steam is pure JSON. Maintenance cost is what kills a 1-vCPU no-browser fleet, not first-week demand.

## Cycle 57 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED: steam-reviews-scraper was silently capped at 100 reviews per app since launch
- **Followed the cycle-56 queued follow-up**: cursor-pagination depth had only ever been smoke-tested at 1 page (10-item test_input). Ran `maxReviewsPerApp: 500` against Hollow Knight (367520) — expected ~5 pages of 100. Got exactly **100 reviews, then the run silently stopped as if the feed had ended.**
- **Root cause: double URL-encoding of the pagination cursor.** `scrapeReviews()` called `reviewsUrl(appId, encodeURIComponent(cursor))`, but `reviewsUrl()` builds the query string with `new URLSearchParams({..., cursor})`, which **already** percent-encodes every value it's given. So from page 2 onward the cursor (which contains `/` and `=`, e.g. `AoJ4gPzjwKADdKjN/wY=`) was encoded twice (`%2F` → `%252F`). Verified directly with curl: a double-encoded cursor makes Steam return `success:1` with **`num_reviews: 0`** and `cursor: null` — indistinguishable from genuinely reaching the end of the feed, which is exactly the condition the loop treats as "done." Page 1 always worked because the initial cursor is the literal `*`, which encodes to itself either way — so this bug was invisible to every prior smoke test (all of which used ≤10-item pulls that never left page 1).
- **Impact: every `reviews`-mode run on the live, published Actor (public since cycle 56, $0.0005/result) has been silently capped at 100 reviews per game since launch**, regardless of a user's `maxReviewsPerApp`/`maxResults` setting — for popular games (hundreds of thousands of reviews) this is a real product defect, not an edge case. No revenue/run history yet (0 external users as of cycle 56), so no refund exposure, but this needed fixing before any real customer hit it.
- **Fix** (`src/main.js`): pass the raw `cursor` into `reviewsUrl()` and let `URLSearchParams` encode it exactly once. One-line change.
- **Verified for real, not just locally**: local run went from 100→**500/500 reviews across multiple pages**, all 500 `reviewId`s unique, 0 nulls. Pushed to the platform (`apify push --force` → build 0.1.2) and re-ran the identical 500-review pull live — `apify call` run `Gb7pBGpdHfiJUYDkB` SUCCEEDED, dataset pulled back via the API confirms **500/500 unique reviewIds, 0 nulls**. Regression-checked the original 10-item `test_input.json` path unchanged (10/10). Confirmed `isPublic:true` and PPE $0.0005/result pricing intact after the push (a plain `apify push` without `isPublic` in the payload does not touch the publish-rate-limit cap — consistent with cycles 20/21/38/39/55).
- **Reusable lesson: `encodeURIComponent()` a value yourself only if the thing that builds the final URL does NOT already encode it — `URLSearchParams`, `got`'s `searchParams` option, and most URL builders already do, and manually pre-encoding on top produces a value that still "works" for simple inputs (plain ASCII, `*`) but silently corrupts anything containing characters the encoder would touch (`/`, `+`, `=`). A single-page smoke test with a trivial cursor value cannot catch this — it only surfaces once you paginate past page 1 with a real, structured cursor/token.** Generalize: any future Actor using cursor/token-based pagination should get at least one real multi-page pull in its test plan, not just a first-page smoke test, before or immediately after publish.

## Cycle 58 (2026-09-10, sonnet-5) — steam-reviews-scraper's first competitor gap-check (published cycle 56, never checked)
